# Capiro Meri Full-Completion and Wiring Closeout Plan

**Prepared:** 21 October 2026. **Mode:** comprehensive read-only source audit and implementation-ready closure plan. **Implementation performed:** none. Repository/code/configuration/branch/PR state was not changed; no builds/tests, deployments, migrations, provider requests, actual mail, credential changes or live writes were performed. Local research snapshots and this document are the only written artifacts.

## Executive verdict

Meri is **not an unwired shell**. Current source already has real authenticated conversation admission, durable events/history, Stop and continuation machinery, selected tool authority, model-budget/proxy enforcement, substantial read adapters, command/approval journals, artifact storage and Memory owners. Candidate11 is on main and adds important conversation/checkpoint continuity. The user's reported October20 first-chat response is a real milestone and is not erased by this audit.

Meri is also **not yet demonstrated as a fully wired, fully qualified end-to-end product**. The remaining work is a mixture of:
1. **Concrete missing or inconsistent user lifecycles:** actual UI controls/receivers, approval dispatch/recovery and post-decision observation, draft/file/tool handoffs, routines management, selected research output recovery, and Stop/cleanup continuity across changed renders.
2. **Source already available but not received on main:** Meri197 contains substantial compatible read/Memory/artifact/public-contract work; frontend115 merged during this audit and is now source-main. Qualify/review the existing source rather than rebuilding it.
3. **Real domain contracts or consumers still missing:** several advertised catalog capabilities, mail selected composition, broader task mutations and governed schedule intents, Memory retention/public-wire prerequisites, attachment custody, accepted research/artifact workflows and optional advanced surfaces.
4. **Intentionally held capabilities:** browser/Bash, destructive retention, certain Memory writes, generated format/executor paths and unattended/Objective features have safety/authority/native gates. Flipping a flag is not closing those gaps.
5. **Native, provider and release evidence:** source/mock/browser-fixture tests are extensive but include known failures and unrun gates. They do not prove the current candidate's image, database, Core/provider path, live two-turn/deploy recovery, actual document quality or representative task performance.

**Full-completion scope confirmed:** this plan reconciles the original October5 Program and Deployment plans, the October8 Forward plan, October9 Greenfield specification, October10 R2 deployment/reliability plan, October15/17 master crosswalk and remaining backlog, and October20 remaining/adjustment plans against current source. Every original or subsequently agreed feature remains visible, including unbuilt and previously deferred capabilities. A deferral or safe runtime exclusion is not a completed feature or a cancellation. Architecture implementation choices may be superseded with evidence; product outcomes cannot be silently discarded.

**Recommended strategy:** preserve the current architecture and first-chat win; reconcile the exact receiver sources with candidate11 and active P1/P2 owners; close source-safety and durable-lifecycle gaps; complete existing consumers; implement the original/agreed missing contracts in dependency order; then qualify the combined source/image/config/schema/profile with separately authorized operator actions. No broad rewrite, duplicate sender, second capability framework or revival of retired legacy tools is proposed.

This is a full closure inventory, not permission to implement or activate. A smaller first release can explicitly hold optional features, but held/deferred rows are not counted as wired. The indexed items below include defects, implementation gaps, owner decisions and qualification gates, not an inflated bug count.

## Reading guide and state vocabulary

- **Implemented-main:** inspected current source contains the owner/handler path. Individual defects may remain; no live success is implied.
- **Available-in-PR:** inspected code exists at the named open head, not default.
- **Missing consumer / contract / source:** a required receiving UI, authoritative shape, bridge, lifecycle or implementation is absent or incompatible.
- **Configuration/activation held:** code is present but disabled or requires named authority, migration, image or operator evidence.
- **Live-unverified:** this audit did not execute that path against deployed services or providers.
- **Intentional unsupported / external / superseded:** preserve the existing safe external owner or accepted exclusion; do not count it as a defect.
- **Owner decision:** semantics, scope or release target need an explicit decision before implementation can be completed.

**P0** is an authority/security/data-integrity or activation prerequisite for the affected capability. **P1** is core user-facing completeness, reliable recovery or qualification. **P2** is later-stage/advanced completeness or a bounded UX improvement. It does not mean an original or agreed feature can be omitted from full completion. These are scheduling priorities, not claims of live compromise or global production outage.

Every ticket states evidence, root cause, bounded changes, dependencies and acceptance. The coverage ledgers track actual routes, controls, public operations, registered tools, domain producers and persistent lifecycles. The final execution section consolidates canonical owners and removes duplicate projects. Scope limits and unknowns are explicit at the end.

## Architecture to preserve

The working architecture is:

1. **Frontend:** ordinary Meri routes and typed consumers; server-only native identity, source pin and feature gates; no direct browser access to internal credentials.
2. **Meri public/control:** application/action-session issuance; current tenant/actor/client/run binding; durable conversation/input/event/lifecycle journals and typed contracts.
3. **Worker/runtime:** accepted run → allocation/claim/lease → prepared task/SDK/CLI execution; bounded lifetime/Stop/currentness, capability profile, checkpoint and durable outputs.
4. **Gateway/domain owner:** discoverable operation from measured installation + current scope; delegated Core read or governed intent/command, with canonical bytes, budget/liability and receipt.
5. **Core and providers:** canonical native identity, live membership/client permissions, retained/current read and domain mutation receivers; mail/calendar/Portfolio/Workspace remain their existing owners.
6. **Return and recovery:** current output authority → durable receipt/event → validated public DTO → UI observation/history/reload. A successful lower-level call alone does not complete the feature.

### Trace requirements by user-visible flow

| Flow | Complete trace that closure must prove |
|---|---|
| Chat / follow-up | UI client+conversation+request identity → same-origin auth proxy → current action session → input admission journal → run/claim/SDK model proxy → durable assistant/event/checkpoint → live stream + paged history + reload |
| Read/search | Prompt/skill → measured discoverable exact operation/version → argument contract/client scope → one-use delegated Core or retained authority → authoritative source/pagination/time semantics → private observation/evidence → budget settlement/current release → citation/UI |
| Task create / approval | Typed intent preparation → durable proposal/command/approval tuple → owner decision/recheck → exact Core execution grant → task.create effect → durable receipt (or UNKNOWN) → intent/task/history refresh; no blind redispatch |
| Task update / schedule | UI/tool proposal → accepted domain authority contract → revision/CAS/consent → existing Core/schedule owner → durable effect + idempotency → background firing/cancel/reconcile and user-visible status; missing owners cannot be replaced with a generic bearer |
| Memory | Recall/proposal source scope → producer/candidate → review/correction/approval → canonical Core Memory effect or safe held response → four payload retention/tombstone linkage → current visibility/retrieval/history |
| Document / research deliverable | Selected input and evidence custody → governed preparation/executor → format-valid/native-usable output → immutable version/object/hash/provenance → current inspect/download/preview → Files/message/history and expiry/retention |
| Attachment / filing | Authorized user bytes → upload/read custody with content limits/scan/tenant-client binding → read/analysis/generation selection → explicit filing intent and Workspace destination → durable receipt/access/reload; ordinary chat text is not attachment support |
| Browser / Bash | Accepted capability+scope and explicit effect authority → canonical isolated host/egress owner → bounded execution+network filesystem policy → result provenance → cleanup/absence proof and current output release. Unmounted host source is not active browser support |
| Cross-module entry | Portfolio/Workspace/Settings selected entity → canonical Meri context contract, not name guessing → real supported action/receipt → receiving module refresh; existing PF/WS/ST/ML owners remain |

## Full product completion versus a limited release

The user clarified that the deliverable must cover the original plan and **every feature needed to fully complete Meri**, not merely the currently mounted pages. The original-plan traceability chapters therefore form an equally binding part of this inventory. They map original decisions, stages, tasks, capabilities, skills, design coverage and deferred feature packets to current source, a concrete closure ticket or a visible unresolved decision.

A small first release may keep a capability safely dark. That is **limited-release acceptance**, not full-product completion. In later sections, phrases such as “if promised,” “optional,” or “owner decision” distinguish safe implementation/activation sequencing or genuinely conditional original requirements. They do not cancel any feature explicitly required by the original-plan crosswalk. Full completion requires those mapped features and their acceptance evidence. A product choice that changes a promised outcome must be explicitly approved and recorded as a scope change, not marked done by an engineer.

The139-capability historical registry, selected96-capability map, current97 descriptors,14 named skills and43 explicit exclusions are different inventories, not interchangeable feature counts. The tools chapter names the current97 descriptors and43 exclusions; the original crosswalk preserves the historical selection and later-added behavior. No broad legacy executor is reactivated merely to match a count.

Fixture-only controls remain non-authoritative, but when they correspond to original or agreed product requirements they have a full-completion receiving ticket. Conversely, a visible action that loses its decision receipt, cannot refresh its effect, or has no authorized downstream owner is not wired merely because a button and endpoint exist.

**Document navigation:** original-plan and later-plan traceability; frontend/control MF; runtime/continuity MR; registry/domain MT; Memory/artifact MM; full-product MO/MB/MG requirements; cross-cutting release MX; consolidated execution and acceptance. The generated index links each closure ticket once.

### Companion plan and shared dependencies

The dated source-state corrections in this document supersede older PR115-open and precursor197-conflict descriptions in the companion; canonical ownership references do not change.

The companion deliverable is **Capiro Portfolio, Workspace, Settings and Mail Wiring Closeout Plan**, prepared21October2026, filename `Capiro-Portfolio-Workspace-Settings-Mail-Wiring-Closeout-Plan-20261021.md`. Its ticket IDs below are references to existing owners, not additional Meri projects. This document restates the needed boundaries so the implementation handoff is usable without earlier conversation.

- X-01–06: ordinary-versus-preview scope, service/auth routing, configuration/image/schema/migration and live receipts.
- PF-02/03: selected-client/profile/file context entry into Meri; canonical IDs and permissions, not display-name inference.
- WS-22–24: Workspace generation entry, Meri artifact public access/version/custody contract and receiving UI; no new storage platform.
- ST-07–11: Memory governance/grants/files/notifications and owner review/correction; one Core owner and current public DTO.
- ST-14–17: account/connection status, authoritative usage/budgets, preferences/model/skill and routine settings; no fake independent authority.
- ML-02–10/16: provider transport, durable send intent, account lineage, reconciliation, threading and authorization; Meri must not bypass these through direct mail calls.
- ML-20/23/24: Timeline/Meri send consumer, newsletter dispatch and separate system notifications; each keeps its own approved effect and recipient contract.

<a id="greenfield-crosswalk"></a>
## Original-plan scope and Greenfield specification

### What “every feature” means in this document

The user explicitly confirmed that this is a plan to fully complete Meri, including the original plan and every feature. This chapter is not a second architecture proposal. It preserves each original/agreed outcome, maps it to the current architecture and bounded tickets, and prevents a safe temporary hold from being mislabeled completion. The following source-plan crosswalks supplement, rather than replace, the current-source ledgers.

Two acceptance labels must never be conflated:

1. **Limited release accepted:** a named, safely bounded capability set has the required source, native, provider and operator receipts. Other named features remain held and visibly incomplete.
2. **Full Meri completion accepted:** every original/agreed requirement has either its complete current acceptance evidence or an explicit user-approved replacement/cancellation with rationale and affected acceptance recorded. A missing implementation, an unmounted registry entry, a deferred feature, an owner decision or an unrun gate is not completion.

Historical plans contain old execution instructions and historical permissions. Those are evidence of intended product behavior, not authorization to run commands, delete resources, send mail, configure credentials or deploy now. All work in this document remains a plan, and future operator actions follow the approval gates below. Historical host paths, account identifiers and old live task definitions are not reused as present deployment inputs.

### Requirement-source provenance and precedence

The original attachments were read in full. Content hashes below identify the exact supplied documents, independent of similarly named copies. The later Greenfield/R2 architecture changes how older outcomes are implemented; it does not automatically erase Cowork, document, drafting, source, approval or operating requirements.

Precedence is evaluated per requirement, not by blindly replacing an entire old document. The later SDK/Temporal plan supersedes old V1/V2/V3 runtime implementation paths. Original business outcomes carry forward into SDK skills, typed Core commands, existing artifact/Memory owners and current UI contracts. A safe exclusion of an old unrestricted tool is not evidence that the associated promised business outcome was cancelled. Historical acceptance assumed complete in a later plan must be traced to an exact applicable receipt before it is credited; do not rerun an obsolete expensive suite merely because its number appears in an older document.

### Quantitative and policy reconciliation rules

- Keep the **60 real-task production benchmark**, original **T1–T18 human-verdict acceptance**, distinct **R2 retrieval programme starting with 75 real requests, expanding to at least 200 labeled cases, with a separate frozen held-out evaluation split**, adversarial suite and visible-control tests separate. They measure different outcomes and must not be added into a decorative test total.
- October20's assumption that an earlier programme is complete is not a newly observed pass. Locate its pinned receipt and determine whether changed code invalidates it. Reuse applicable receipts; run only affected/new acceptance under newly approved provider cost/data scope. Historical “do not rebuild the retired giant benchmark” is not permission to omit deterministic security tests or a later explicitly adopted R2 programme.
- Do not let an optional alternative such as Managed Agents, hosted proprietary skills, a separate AWS account, fine-tuning or a new provider default become an unrequested mandatory rewrite. Preserve conditional decision gates and their evidence, while retaining explicit original product promises as work still to complete.
- The source/line evidence below establishes what exists and what is missing. No full-completion status can be derived from source alone. A final acceptance register must resolve every GF/original/intermediate/latest row and every closure ticket with the exact release evidence or an explicit approved product-scope change.

<a id="original-crosswalk"></a>
## Original Program and Deployment requirements

### Full completion scope, not a first-chat launch checklist

#### Purpose and governing distinction

This crosswalk preserves the original product promises while mapping them onto the current greenfield architecture and named closeout tickets. **A changed implementation architecture may replace V3 paths, tables, provider adapters or release scripts; it does not silently remove the behavior promised to the user.** “Held”, “source exists”, “behind a flag”, “fixture” and “candidate PR” are not completed features. A mandatory original feature is done only when the ordinary user workflow and its acceptance evidence are complete, or the user explicitly changes that requirement.

The user's latest clarification is full Meri completion, including every feature, rather than only first chat or an initial preview. The first successful Fable chat reported on 20 October remains a valid milestone. It is not claimed to satisfy the Cowork, file, proactive, drafting or provider-comparison program.

This is a read-only traceability artifact. No tests, provider calls, production actions, code changes, merges, deletions or external communications were performed. Historical instructions inside the original documents are not fresh authorization to execute their old release commands.

#### Original sources inspected in full

- **P** = `00-Meri-Program-Plan-2026-10-05.md`, 331 lines, local preserved source SHA-256 `0a5a1081a5facdc2e2fdeda7e628ce512295b230eb2e2324469088227186ce53`.
- **D** = `00-Meri-Deployment-Plan-Opus-5-5-2026-10-05.md`, 324 lines, local preserved source SHA-256 `5b2d3bad25298f3f081da1bc09d00e703174f9c858e6ed1c3afe05d0511a47a9`.
- References such as **P §5.2 L189–191** below identify exact original sections/lines, not current source positions.
- Current evidence C01–C34 is pinned below. The domain audit files contain deeper line-level evidence and implementation detail. FE citations at `4f9a7e8dcaa9ba03494dc5a49559daf70cea5100` are audited unchanged lineage: the FE auditor reconciled the later merge to main `24316f913e6082cf33fb0049c1187dc64097975f`. Do not infer current PR state from an older source link.
- MO tickets are owned once by the tools/full-scope author. This crosswalk **does not create duplicate MO work**. MR/MF/MT/MM/MX retain their existing owners; MB is the browser/device/source-trigger companion scope.

#### Status key

- **Required/open:** original mandatory feature remains full-completion work.
- **Source partial / qualification open:** some implementation exists; exact original workflow is not yet evidenced complete.
- **Architecture superseded:** replace the old mechanism, retain the stated outcome.
- **Conditional:** the original explicitly required a trigger or user decision. Record the trigger/decision, not a silent deletion.
- **Historical fact, reverify:** old production IDs, status, versions, costs and commands cannot establish current readiness.

### 5. Named supplementary full-scope owners and boundaries

The MO source author owns the following additions once; they are referenced here, not re-authored:

- MO-01 versioned built-in/firm procedures.
- MO-02 durable Cowork job/child goal-revision integration over existing runtime.
- MO-03 exact federal drafting engine and revision-bound workbench.
- MO-04 governed communication send/reply through canonical Mail owners.
- MO-05 full Research Portfolio operational workflows.
- MO-06 domain watch subscribe/unsubscribe and reviewed comparisons/relevance.
- MO-07 graph-path/PE buyer/funding analysis semantics.
- MO-08 prospect/client/note governed mutations.
- MO-09 global/tenant disable and restore, including alerts.
- MO-10 complete-job cost/context/fairness, extending P1/MX-09 rather than replacing them.
- MO-11 safe legacy code/config retirement.
- MO-12 Hermes cloud residue retirement.
- MO-13 provider comparison/default disposition.
- MO-14 source-change dependency refresh and scoped feedback lineage.
- MO-15 named connected-source capability completion under actual entitlement/scopes.

MB-01 takeover, MB-02 authenticated sites, MB-03 device bridge, MB-04 natural-language selected-version artifact revision and MB-05 supported source-event trigger bridge belong to the memory/artifact/browser author. Full Office editing remains MM-20. All fourteen declared skills are a full-completion MT-13 requirement, not a permanently optional subset. MR-03/04/10 owns routine management/consent/firing; MO-06 owns business watch subscription; MB-05 owns source-event ingress/dedup bridge; MO-14 owns downstream source-revision invalidation. These are distinct lifecycle stages, not four schedulers.

Original Program §2.1 lists connector behaviors, not a permission to connect every tenant to every service. MO-15 must inventory each named source as entitled-and-qualified, unavailable-for-this-account, or explicitly superseded by a recorded user decision. An unsupported operation cannot simply disappear because it is absent from the currently installed catalog.

### 6. Completion rule and outstanding decisions

For each required feature above, the integrated plan must retain:
1. Original requirement reference.
2. Current implementation owner and exact contract/source.
3. Missing producer, consumer, authority, custody, background/recovery or operational step.
4. A bounded ticket/dependency and ordinary-user acceptance.
5. Source/merged/configured/deployed/live-verdict status.
6. An explicit user-approved supersession if the feature is removed or materially changed.

**Full completion is not** “all launch flags enabled”, “all existing endpoints callable”, “all current source tests green”, “first chat succeeded”, or “fixtures look finished”. It includes the accepted yet-unbuilt product behavior.

Decisions still needing explicit reconciliation, not unilateral omission:
- Provider/default/comparison promise versus later SDK/Bedrock implementation constraints.
- Original small-test/verdict policy versus later greenfield safety/release evidence requirements. Reuse existing checks and keep reference benchmarks out of the mandatory path unless a later instruction explicitly requires them.
- Conditional writing profile/roles/fine-tuning and deferred drafting variants. Preserve conditions and outcome, rather than inventing mandatory training or claiming generic drafting fulfills exact-text work.
- Exact legacy/Hermes retirement completion, pending developer-tooling use, and current cloud references.
- Any additional behavior in the original referenced annexes not enumerated by the canonical Program text. Keep an unresolved requirement row until the source is inspected or a deliberate supersession is recorded.

<a id="r2-forward-crosswalk"></a>
## Forward plan and R2 reliability/design requirements

Prepared 21 October 2026. This is a requirements-to-work crosswalk, not an implementation or release receipt. It retains original features, including not-yet-built features, while avoiding a duplicate architecture or a false claim that existing source is operational.

### 1. Sources, precedence and scope

Two complete supplied originals were read:

- **R2**: `Meri_Codex_Hermes_Deployment_Plan.md`, 665 lines, SHA-256 `43f9be7c385a1e321dbc9721bd7ff97752031a62e661f7691b78e0a20fd7c388`. Revision R2, 10 October 2026. This is byte-identical to the R2 master identified in the historical crosswalk at [immutable master](https://github.com/neo-capiro/meri-greenfield/blob/8b123bea4c90794dc2d766814b6ddbdbfe0928bc/docs/plans/MERI-DEPLOYMENT.md). References below use R2 line numbers.
- **Forward**: `Meri-Forward-Execution-Plan-2026-10-08.md`, 283 lines, SHA-256 `67f66da09202fcda947a1aca01637444c4dde850e2ca1eb5cd7de16cdea9e436`. References below use Forward line numbers. This is a historical source document, not a claim that its inspected October 8 deployment remains current.

Current work mapping uses frontend `24316f913e6082cf33fb0049c1187dc64097975f` (PR115 already merged), Meri main `bce707763f2cbabe1a73a99aee7b7ec206fa6996`, Meri supplemental PR197 `cf634d4f70376c12ee6dd7b97ec22981dbd4cb2a`, and Core `backend-import@309af848f1aa403f6c8e54aa0d22990d4230f063`. Their detailed source evidence is in the MF/MR/MT/MM/MX audit sections. No runtime/provider action, test execution, repository change or deployment was performed for this crosswalk.

**Authority reconciliation, not scope reduction:**

1. The latest user explicitly asks for every feature needed to fully complete Meri. A first-chat milestone, two installed skills, a small canary or a limited deployment is not full completion. Every original feature is either mapped to an existing work item, explicitly added below, or marked as a conditional technology/adoption decision rather than silently omitted.
2. R2 is later than Forward and explicitly supersedes conflicting earlier architecture/routing instructions. The current standalone Claude Agent SDK loop, independent Meri backend and accepted consolidated Capiro frontend remain the architecture. Forward's behavioral promises survive; its old V3/OpenAI-loop implementation instructions do **not** authorize rebuilding or restoring the retired runtime.
3. Forward says retired 75-case capture/grading gates must not be resurrected. R2 later introduces a **different, explicitly retained reliability protocol**: start with 75 representative requests, expand to at least 200 labelled cases, isolate development/calibration from frozen held-out data, and run routing/evidence oracles. These are not a license for the retired capture framework, mandatory LLM grading or per-case release kits.
4. **The R2 reliability cohort does not replace the original at least 60 representative end-to-end task suite.** Keep both sets of definitions, denominators and results. Cases may overlap only where labels and scoring independently satisfy both purposes. Do not call all 75 initial cases “held-out” without a frozen split; held-out cases must not be used for tuning.
5. Historical selected/deferred capability and mobile decisions remain evidence of staged rollout, not silent completion. The full-completion programme must retain those deferred features with implementation/decision gates. It must not flip permissions, activate browser/device access or enable external effects just because the user requests a comprehensive plan.
6. Optional Decisions/JEV, Code Connect, alternative hosting, retrieval donors and fine-tuning remain conditional. Full completion means a recorded tested adoption/disabled decision where the original plan made the technology optional, not mandatory production dependency on every named tool.
7. Historical Codex/Hermes lane/session counts and AWS shipping assignments are process instructions governed by newer ownership. Preserve their useful constraints (single integration owner, bounded changes, independent review, no duplicate dispatcher, no unauthorized deployment), not a second product runtime.

#### 3.1 Explicit catalogue/skill/evidence schema completion checklist

Source: R2 lines235–274. Existing versioned contracts are reused and extended only for demonstrated missing fields.

| Contract area | Required fields/behavior | Owner and closure evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Capability descriptor | Stable ID/version, authority, selection examples/limitations, closed input/output schema, aliases, side effects, grants, handler, discovery terms, retry/error taxonomy, replacement map, R/REL/RG and fixture IDs | MT-01/16; schema-handler-description drift and unavailable-operation lint against installed runtime. |
| Retrieval | Authorized entity resolution, calendar/FY/version normalization, exact/search/read distinction, source inventory, authoritative/derived/approved-memory distinctions, continuation and complete-negative policy | MT-16/MG-05/06/MM-17; deterministic exactness and partial-coverage cases. |
| Skill manifest | Immutable ID/version, required evidence, legal operations, typed subject/window, output/artifact rubric, completion, budget, approval and existing-run version policy | MT-13/MG-02/MM-15; full workflow cannot pass because prose exists. |
| Logical skill invocation | skill_id, skill_version, scope_request(kind/entity_id), window_request(basis/expression/timezone or explicit bounds), prompt; normalized server-returned scope/window and pinned manifest | MG-02; exact value continuity and explicit preserve/replace decision after changed goal. |
| Evidence outcome | complete/partial/empty/unavailable/denied/error; stable reason and retryability | MT-16/MM-17; no denied/unavailable converted to empty. |
| Evidence request | request/trace ID, operation/version, resolved scope, normalized date/FY/version and applied filters | MT-16/MG-02/05; bind each read to exact request and current authority. |
| Evidence authority/method | source/system, authoritative/derived/approved-memory, exact/search/read, source snapshot/version and adapter version | MM-17/MT-16; never flatten approved memory into official facts. |
| Evidence material | Stable record IDs, authorized locators/citations, actual passage/full-read handle, snippet versus full-read | MM-12/13/17/MF-16; table headers/qualifiers/footnotes preserved. |
| Evidence coverage | Requested/checked/unavailable sources, enumerable count only when known, returned count, truncation, cursor/page/range/history limits | MT-16/MM-17; vector top-k never gets invented total; >10-record histories use completeness, not Recall@10. |
| Freshness/continuation | Source synchronization/as-of, retrieval time, stale/unknown; authorized cursor/read handles and mandatory followup | MG-06/MO-14/MT-16; stale retries cannot overwrite fresh revision, revoked handle cannot release bytes. |
| Evaluation/release records | Frozen split/labels/scoring/oracle protocol, budgets, optional adoption decision and exact candidate evidence | MG-01/04/MX-01/09/10; original suite stays separate. |

### 8. Six additional bounded gap tickets

These are the requirements not explicit enough in the source-wiring tickets to claim whole-original-plan coverage. They add no new agent loop, database, generic evaluation platform or orchestration framework. They reference original IDs and reuse current source/owners. “Open/unproven” means current composed acceptance is not established, not that no helper code exists anywhere.

<a id="mg-01"></a>
#### MG-01 — Complete the retained R2 reliability corpus and diagnostic protocol

**Priority:** P1 full-product qualification; aliases R09/R10, REL01/06/09, core RG01–09/12. **Root cause:** MX10 retains60 real tasks but does not by itself preserve the separate75→200 reliability scope, held-out split, atomic Recall@10 constraints or correct-tool/correct-evidence oracle protocol.

**Bounded work:** Reuse existing `evals/` fixtures, Promptfoo adapters, OpenTelemetry, R2 requirements/tickets/gates ledgers and current source packet schemas. Freeze75 initial representative requests, expand to≥200 versioned labelled cases, and group near-duplicates/source snapshots before development/calibration/held-out split. Retain exact source snapshots, authorized subject/window, accepted routes/arguments, gold evidence/claims and allowed clarification/abstention. Label source absent versus unindexed/unretrieved/not-read/not-used. Run original path, then same-authority correct-tool/argument oracle, then gold-authorized-evidence oracle. Keep redacted per-stage trace/call/token/cost/latency and human verdict. Scope implementation to missing fixture/adjudication adapters rather than a new grading system.

**Dependencies:** MT source/skill implementation, MM evidence lineage, MX08–11, current model/data/cost permission for later paid qualification. **Acceptance:** All R/RG rows have denominators, source slices, confidence intervals where claimed and immutable candidate results; route≥95%, eligible Recall@10≥95%, exact deterministic cases100%, false absence0, and all core gates explicitly pass/fail/not-run. More-than10gold histories score enumeration/completeness, never altered gold counts. The original60task suite still independently meets≥90%completion/≥95%fact correctness. No tuning on held-out cases and no scripted-provider result counted as live quality.

<a id="mg-02"></a>
#### MG-02 — Preserve selected skill, scope and normalized window across every UI lifecycle path

**Priority:** P1 original-scope consumer; aliases R03/R07/R08, REL04, RG02/RG06/RG12. **Root cause:** Real chat input contracts/journals already carry some selected metadata, but no complete ordinary launcher consumer/receipt demonstrates every original skill/version/window through all lifecycle actions. MF18 generic template work is insufficient without these explicit tests.

**Bounded files:** Existing frontend composer/launcher/setup components, `shared/meri-conversations` initial-input/query/turn journals and new lifecycle adapter from MF10; existing Meri `packages/contracts` skill/window schemas, `control/conversations/skills/admission.mjs`, `worker/conversation/skills/selected-runtime.mjs` and current manifests owned by MT13. No parallel skill registry.

**Work:** Expose14 named launcher identities and exact available versions with disabled reasons until qualified. Submit stable skill ID/version plus typed scope/window, not just expanded prompt text. Server resolves entity and exact timestamps/timezone/week/FY/version, pins immutable manifest and returns displayable normalized receipt. Preserve receipt through reload/reconnect/steer/resume/fork or require an explicit reviewed version/goal change; do not silently pick latest. Handle selected client versus named other subject, mixed/portfolio domains, chosen dates conflicting with text, unavailable sources and current grant loss. Ordinary free chat must not become a comprehensive skill by keyword.

**Acceptance:** Every14skill workflow tests exact identity/window at submit, steering, lost-response recovery, reload, reconnect, resume and fork; mismatched manifest/hash/version rejected; source families cannot be dropped by memory keyword or single-family routing. DST/week/FY/version edge fixtures pass100%; prompts remain draft until explicit send; no capability widening. MT13 owns installed runtime and MM15 owns output, not this consumer.

<a id="mg-03"></a>
#### MG-03 — Reconcile the exact original Figma nodes and every visible control to live acceptance

**Priority:** P1 full-UI completeness; aliases W06/W09 and original design criteria. **Root cause:** The 258fixture-route census and659control-site index prove source coverage, not that the original85frames/29overlays/60states/15component families/11walkthroughs were correctly implemented or explicitly staged. Actual current design revision/node mapping was not retrieved in this audit.

**Bounded work:** Existing `docs/UI-SPEC.md`, `docs/UI-COVERAGE.md`, `docs/design/meri/`, frontend existing components/routes and Playwright suites. At authorized implementation discovery, pin actual Figma revision/capture/frame IDs/reference screenshots; map each original and revised node to route/component/stable control ID, transition, public API/event or local-only behavior, actor/scope/exact-version rules, states and tests. Record discrepancies and explicit original-scope decisions, not silently remove frames. Reuse AI Elements/shadcn and approved shells; no screenshot-based app or new UI QA platform.

**Acceptance:**100% original accepted frames/overlays/states/controls accounted; full-completion queue includes deferred phone/tablet designs, not a fake mobile PASS. All11walkthroughs become real fixture-backed interaction paths plus applicable composed flows. Fix1440desktop/390phone and discovered tablet references/fonts/clock/dynamic content; keyboard/focus/return/scroll/contrast/reduced-motion/long-content tests pass, no critical overlaps or missing action. Three mode buttons preserve authorized context or truthful disabled target. Backend proof remains required separately; no visual mock closes a workflow ticket.

<a id="mg-04"></a>
#### MG-04 — Preserve optional Decisions/JEV qualification without making either a dependency

**Priority:** P2 conditional technology evaluation; aliases R11/R12, REL07/08, RG10/11. **Root cause:** Generic verifier/model-comparison tickets do not explicitly retain bounded routing advice versus evidence-judge roles, shadow isolation and separate adoption gates.

**Work A:** After accepted SDK baseline, check current supported Decisions API/model/account and data-handling permissions. If available and selected for evaluation, implement one bounded trusted-tier choice/predicate/score call after authority filtering, with multi-family/clarify/abstain output. It cannot create arguments, execute tools, alter scope or remove manifest-required sources. Paired held-out shadow compares routing errors, calibrated confidence, latency and outage fallback. **Work B:** Existing JEV verifier owner uses separately labelled relevance/support/client-fit cases and task-specific frozen improvement/calibration/abstention threshold. Protect exact authoritative counts/status and mandatory evidence. Neither advisor can veto baseline required source reads; a future JEV router requires a separate decision.

**Bounded files:** Existing trusted model/verification adapter seams, current eval fixtures/result records, independent optional flags/kill switches; no browser direct calls or additional production agent loop. **Dependencies:** MG01 baseline, MO13 provider seam, MX09 cost/latency and explicit optional data/cost authorization. **Acceptance:** Decisions promotion only if≥25%relative held-out routing-error reduction with no material coverage/argument regression, sufficient paired evidence/CI, addedp95≤500ms and totalp95regression≤10%. JEV passes frozen task-specific quality/calibration/no-required-evidence-loss and latency bounds. Timeout/outage/unavailable/confidence rejection returns accepted baseline. If evidence insufficient or tool unavailable, record disabled/shadow decision and keep core release moving. No mandatory chain of two classifiers.

<a id="mg-05"></a>
#### MG-05 — Deliver automatic canonical client context to ordinary chat

**Priority:** P1 full everyday-client requirement; alias Forward B1 and R02/R04/R08. **Root cause:** A callable get_client_context tool and client-scoped navigation do not prove ordinary chat receives canonical facts automatically. Existing audit establishes the read tool, not the complete automatic context path. Treat implementation presence as to-be-reconciled, not presumed absent.

**Bounded owner/files:** Current Core canonical client/profile/engagement read owner and its existing authorized adapter; Meri current conversation input/context assembly and selected/native read path; no imported old Context Plane, recursive public-tool call or new profile store. Reuse current schema/receipt provenance and caches only if actor+tenant+client+relevant revision keyed. MT provides exact canonical reader paths; MM01/17 preserve memory/provenance.

**Work:** Assemble a compact, task-appropriate authorized context packet before ordinary model work: identity/exact identifiers; capabilities/mission; objectives/active matters; constraints/exclusions; footprint/people; open commitments and critical approved decisions/corrections. Proposed2–4Ktoken budget is a measured start, not an immutable cap or mandatory full history fetch. Include source IDs/revisions/as-of/count-or-sample semantics and explicit gaps. Resolve selected versus explicit other client/portfolio/external subject server-side; do not let selection hijack the user's question. Separate canonical facts, approved shared memory, user preferences and ephemeral conversation. Current instruction overrides stylistic defaults, not source truth.

**Acceptance:** Rich/sparse profiles, paraphrases, selected-client switch, explicit other subject, same-name ambiguity, approved correction in new thread and denied wall sections. Critical seeded identity/constraints/corrections are correct or clearly conflicted/unavailable; six profile sections available without model choosing a tool. Source/permission changes invalidate context before release. Measure actual token/call/latency effect; no full database dump or stale-client leak. Links to MF19 context handoff and MG01 corpus, not a replacement for them.

<a id="mg-06"></a>
#### MG-06 — Make source indexing freshness revision-durable and visible

**Priority:** P1 data reliability; aliases Forward B3 and R04/R05. **Root cause:** Current source inventory/read adapters do not alone prove a durable indexing lifecycle or truthful pending/failed/searchable state. Forward's old setImmediate observation must be reproduced against current Core before changing code; it is not a current defect claim.

**Bounded work:** Existing Core document/KB indexer, Postgres/pgvector rows and durable job/outbox owner; existing Meri evidence freshness envelope; small UI/source-read status consumer. Inspect exact save→index intent→worker→searchable receipt ownership; repair only missing durable seams. Persist source revision-bound intent, retry/failure/searchable status; idempotent update/delete/reassign/revoke, monotonic version guard preventing old retry overwrite. Primary authoritative save remains successful while index pending; profiles/approved memory remain current via canonical reads. Restricted/private documents remain excluded from broader shared indexing and are read by exact authorized paths.

**Dependencies:** MO14 source-change invalidation/refresh/feedback, MM12/13 extraction/custody, MT16 result semantics, MG05 canonical context. This is the underlying index-intent/freshness subtask, not a second MO14 event pipeline or new vector database. **Acceptance:** Crash/restart/temporary failure/retry, source delete/reassign/revoke, stale retry and extraction backlog show correct visible state; old indexed content cannot reappear after revoke. Proposed ordinary-supported-document searchablep95≤5min measured before SLA commitment; failed/excluded sources have explicit status. Answer cannot claim source absence because index is pending, nor wait indefinitely for irrelevant connectors. Unknown current source implementation is resolved by bounded owner discovery first.

### 9. Completion and residual unknowns

This crosswalk preserves all12W packages,12R requirements,9REL tickets,12RG gates,18original quantitative release criteria, the complete Figma/UI and production-boundary contract, all8Forward packets and its retained decisions/acceptance/release promises. It adds6bounded MG tickets rather than treating broader existing ticket titles as proof.

Remaining unknowns are concrete: current design revision/node mapping; complete real source snapshots and consented model corpus; automatic context assembly's actual current seam; current Core durable index/outbox status; optional service availability/calibration; original stricter performance cohort definitions; and composed deployment qualification. None is silently declared implemented. Current source references in domain audits establish what exists; these original-plan obligations establish what still must be proven or built.

No implementation, provider use, test run, deployment, credential or external-agent contact was performed. This is the complete planning crosswalk requested for consolidation with the Greenfield, October5/17/20 and present-source reports.

Prepared 21 October 2026. Read-only reconciliation of the 15/17 October master/backlog against the 21 October implementation audit. This supplements, rather than replaces, the original-plan D/T matrix, the Oct20 MO extension and canonical PF/WS/ST/ML tickets.

### Decision and important corrections

The completion target is every mandatory original Meri requirement and every subsequently accepted feature, including accepted work sequenced after the first shipment. First-chat success, a source merge, an intentionally disabled button or a deferred milestone is not full completion. Retain the four advanced features and responsive/mobile work in the full-program ledger; implementation order and activation authority remain separate.

Five previously under-bounded gaps now have explicit tickets: MB-01 takeover, MB-02 authenticated sites, MB-03 device/browser bridge, MB-04 natural-language selected-version revision and MB-05 source-event triggers. Full Office editing stays MM-20. All 14 skills stay MT-13, not an optional two-skill cohort. Required scanned-image OCR stays MM-12 using the adopted extractor. Mobile stays MF-20 as a held later milestone with explicit acceptance, not silently removed.

The 43 excluded/deferred legacy operations are still 43 explicitly classified rows, not 43 newly authorized unrestricted mutations. Distinguish a required modern user journey from an excluded legacy alias, and retain the exact approved bounded replacement or an open scope decision. Optional Decisions/JEV remain independent evaluated/shadow adoption options; the original baseline must work without them. Do not weaken these boundaries because the user asked for a complete plan.

#### Original quantitative and operational criteria: all 18 retained

These acceptance obligations are part of the cited owners, not a new test platform. “Or change the threshold later” is not the current completion criterion. Preserve the frozen original targets and report blockers rather than lowering denominators.

| Criterion | Required acceptance | Canonical ticket(s) |
|---|---|---|
| Real work | Preserve the original at least 60 representative end-to-end tasks with at least 90% successful completion and at least 95% checked factual correctness. Start reliability work with 75 real requests and expand to at least 200 labelled cases; score the applicable frozen held-out RG gates separately | MX-10 |
| Cross-tenant isolation | Zero unauthorized rows, citations, transcripts, browser state or files across at least 100 adversarial cases | MX-11 |
| Within-tenant authorization | 100% fixtures pass for client walls, mailbox ownership, private memory, expired grants, role changes and revocation of already-stored context | MX-11; MT-16; MM-01 |
| Revocation | Numeric propagation bound agreed before G3 and met in staging; live cancellation, further publication denial and historical thread/file/preview/artifact access checks pass | MX-11; MR-05/08; MB-01/02 |
| Source coverage | 100% agreed source inventory has authorized reads; counts/aggregates match fixtures; pagination/history correct; citations authorized; freshness and completeness disclosed | MT-01–09/16; MM-17 |
| Current Meri scope | Every selected capability has a new implementation and passing behavior/policy fixture; deferred items explicitly recorded | MT-01/13/15; MX-01 |
| Native artifacts | 100% benchmark files open; supported formulas and reference totals correct; at least 95% meet the agreed human quality rubric; zero critical clipping/corruption; originals preserved | MM-18; MB-04 |
| Recovery and side effects | 100% restart/retry suite passes; recovery within two minutes on healthy infrastructure; zero duplicate mutations after repeated delivery or worker termination | MR-06–10; MX-12 |
| User interface | 100% distinct visible controls for the accepted scope have mapped behavior and passing checks, including stop, steer, resume, fork, approve/reject, citations, upload, preview, versions, revision, exact-version download, filing and schedules; disabled controls cannot execute | MF-01–21; MM-08–20; MB-01–05 |
| Design traceability | 100% Figma baseline frames, overlays and state examples are classified and mapped to implementation/fixtures or an explicit existing scope deferral; zero silently omitted agreed v1 screens or states | MF-20/21; original design ledger |
| Visual and responsive behavior | Approved desktop/tablet/phone and light/dark references reviewed against the staging build; zero critical clipping, overlaps, unreadable content or missing actions; all accepted visual differences recorded | MF-20/21; held mobile milestone |
| Interaction and accessibility | Keyboard navigation, accessible names, visible focus, dialog focus/return, contrast, long content/scrolling and reduced-motion behavior pass; selected scope and exact artifact/version are preserved through actions | MF-20; ST-13 |
| Mode switch | Meri active; Portfolio/Modules content absent from this workstream; configured shared-shell handoffs preserve authorized tenant/client context, or unavailable destinations are explicitly disabled and tested | MF-19; PF-02/03; WS-22 |
| Browser operation | Authentication, cancellation, uploads/downloads, restart, isolation and revocation pass for each enabled browser workflow | MT-14; MB-01/02; MM-12/13 |
| Performance and cost | At 10 concurrent runs, p95 UI acknowledgement at most two seconds and p95 fresh-task readiness at most 90 seconds; tenant budgets, concurrency and runtime limits enforced; 100% runs/attempts have measured usage, including failed/cancelled/retried work | MX-09; MR-09; original P1/P2 |
| Data lifecycle | Retention/deletion fixtures pass across transcripts, events, uploads, browser state, snapshots, previews and artifacts; documented backup/restore rules prevent renewed access | MR-05; MM-02/07/10/12; MX-12 |
| Deployment operations | Exact candidate succeeds in staging; alerting, restore, rollback and in-flight compatibility drills pass | MX-06–08/12/14 |
| Reuse | Every generic subsystem has a pinned adoption/license record; custom replacements explain a demonstrated limitation | MX-05; original component-adoption register |

Additional exact P17 §10/RG targets remain: ≥95% accepted routing, 100% subject/window/FY/version and exact count/status/comparison fixtures, Evidence Recall@10 ≥95% for the defined ≤10-gold units, zero false absence on incomplete coverage, 100% all14 skill contracts, real-proxy discovery/compaction/resume, and lifecycle/full-evidence coverage. Retain separately the warm simple-lookup p95 ≤15s and any stricter accepted ordinary-answer ≤8s cohort; neither replaces 10-concurrent acknowledgement ≤2s/fresh readiness ≤90s. Optional router adoption requires ≥25% relative error reduction with power/confidence/coverage protections and ≤500ms incremental/≤10% regression budgets; JEV requires frozen calibrated quality improvement with no required-evidence loss.

#### Shared/deployment/first-chat requirements remain dependencies

- P17 F1 L169–183 maps MF-01/02/07/08/21 and MX-02/05/14: all nine first-chat steps in §8 remain exact current-cohort checks. Shared shell PF/WS/ST/ML requirements stay in the prior companion plan. P17 F2/F3/C1/C3 are not newly cloned Meri projects.
- P17 §§1–4 retain architecture, authorized source ownership, native component adoption, context-free handoff records and one owner per shared contract/migration/lockfile. Historical Hermes/session instructions are not product features; current repo ownership supersedes them.
- P17 §7 Stages0–6 and rollback map MX-01/02/04–07/12/14. All source-to-image/config/migration/network/material/cost/readback/rollback objects must be refreshed, not replayed from old ARNs, priorities, counts or snapshots. Never recreate a resource because a historical checkbox is open.
- The seven stage packets retain backup/restore constraints; executable one-shot migrations and narrow roles; KMS/secret/network custody; count-zero immutable services; exact host denial and shared listener ownership; private native identity readiness; public binding/DNS and authenticated UI proof; forward-safe rollback preserving in-flight data. These are future separately authorized operator actions, not work performed by this plan.
- P17 §9 narrow safety tests while editing and broad tests at coherent milestones maps execution test tiers/MX-13. It does not waive security checks or permit repeated identical failing/paid runs.
- P17 §§11–12 blockers/handoff are MX-01/14 operating records. Old budgets, old denied blobs and historical live states neither become current authority nor require unauthorized retry. Resolve only legitimate dependent blockers; continue independent authorized source work.

### 3. Selected, excluded and named-skill preservation

P17 Appendix A L726–826 has 96 selected names; Appendix B L827–874 has 43 explicit exclusions; Appendix C L875–895 has 14 original skill IDs. H15 machine crosswalk carries every one. The current tools report’s per-operation ledger and section5 exclusion table are the canonical per-item disposition, avoiding a second inconsistent inventory. Current97 descriptors/86 reads/11 governed operations and historical82 read-adapter rows have different denominators; do not subtract one from another to invent parity.

All14 skill IDs are mandatory full-program workflows: research_company, relationship_brief, client_changes, portfolio_changes, portfolio_overlap, mail_history, meeting_prep, commitments_audit, funding_opportunity_match, pe_deep_dive, bill_watch, hearing_watch, representation_lookup, regulatory_docket_brief. MT-13 owns all14; MM-15–17/MB-04 own output lineage and revision where promised. A valid manifest or two currently selected recipes does not close the other12. Source availability alone does not authorize the43 excluded specialized mutations.

### 4. Bounded missing-feature tickets

The five tickets below are required full-program backlog. Sequencing after first shipment remains valid; it is not an optional omission. P0 safety prerequisites apply before activation, and P2 denotes later sequence, not waiver.

<a id="mb-01"></a>
#### MB-01 · Required advanced browser takeover lifecycle (P2 sequence; P0 before exposure)

**Requirement/evidence.** P17 M9 L381–392; H15 W07; original Greenfield viewer/takeover L139. Existing [takeover activation and all nine prerequisites](https://github.com/neo-capiro/meri-greenfield/blob/bce707763f2cbabe1a73a99aee7b7ec206fa6996/apps/worker/conversation/browser/session/activation.mjs#L1-L23) explicitly say REQUIRED after the initial live version. [Browser preconditions and native measurements](https://github.com/neo-capiro/meri-greenfield/blob/bce707763f2cbabe1a73a99aee7b7ec206fa6996/apps/worker/conversation/browser/ACTIVATION-CHECKLIST.md#L1-L97) remain separate from a viewer fixture.

**Root cause.** Real session fence/confidentiality/destruction source exists, but a fully mounted current-authority session owner, authenticated relay/viewer, lifecycle/public controls and same-path native qualification are not established. The activation gate correctly remains held. Public browsing is a prerequisite, not takeover completion.

**Bounded implementation.** Extend the existing browser/session owner, control lifecycle and frontend browser receiver, not a second browser or screencast engine. Use the adopted Playwright monitoring/connection facility. Complete takeover.begin/end as pause-family controls: drain agent actions, claim one human lease, enforce single execution fence, apply confidentiality filtering and route only current authorized frames/input. Viewer tickets must be single-use, ≤60s, and bound to tenant/actor/conversation/run/session-owner instance; never place persistent credentials in links. End/reconnect/expiry retires the paused attempt exactly and resumes only with fresh authority. Network namespace must not reach owner socket or worker-control channel. Preserve cleanup UNKNOWN plus verified host-destruction recovery, source/current withdrawal and truthful UI states.

**Files/owners.** Existing apps/worker/conversation/browser/session/{activation,fence,confidentiality,destruction,input}.mjs; browser runtime/lifecycle; existing control lifecycle/authority owners; one reviewed public takeover DTO extension under packages/contracts; capiro-frontend browser/session hosts and tests. Contract names/migrations allocated centrally. MR owns pause/resume/retirement, MF owns controls, MT-14/MX own browser host.

**Acceptance/tests.** Two tenants/tabs/viewers, expired/replayed tickets, stale owner instance, simultaneous agent/human input, lost begin/end ACK, reconnect during drain, session-owner replacement, revoked membership/client/feature, sign-in fields never leaking into agent view/logs, interrupted cleanup and destruction receipt. Run existing S1 tests1–8, S2 tests1–5/7, S3 tests1–6 plus real viewer lifecycle on the same qualified D005 launch path. No agent action while human lease owns execution; no stale frame/input after revocation. Display drain, paused, reconnecting, reset and UNKNOWN distinctly.

**Dependencies/unknowns.** MT-14, MR-05–08, MF-18, MX-05/11/12; approved isolated-host placement and exact withdrawal bound/evidence. Existing source specifies 30s propagation/staleness for this gate, which must be reconciled with the accepted cross-product bound, not silently loosened. No host, credential or browser activation is authorized by this plan.

<a id="mb-02"></a>
#### MB-02 · Required individually authorized authenticated-site workflows (P2 sequence; P0 confidentiality/effect gates)

**Requirement/evidence.** P17 M9 L385–391. Current [site manifest, empty registry and activation requirements](https://github.com/neo-capiro/meri-greenfield/blob/bce707763f2cbabe1a73a99aee7b7ec206fa6996/apps/worker/conversation/browser/site-workflows/manifest.mjs#L1-L32) provide substantive preparation, not enabled sites. [Credential-free validation and one-site policy narrowing](https://github.com/neo-capiro/meri-greenfield/blob/bce707763f2cbabe1a73a99aee7b7ec206fa6996/apps/worker/conversation/browser/site-workflows/manifest.mjs#L39-L113) already define the contract direction.

**Root cause.** No named first sites/tasks are approved; current ordinary browser destroys run-local state after each run. Required authenticated workflow state across runs, takeover sign-in, per-site legal/privacy checks, durable approvals and measured confidential destruction are incomplete. Broad “browser enabled” is not site qualification.

**Bounded implementation.** Finish the existing platform-owned versioned site manifest and per-conversation session lifecycle. Obtain a finite site/task list and legal/terms review per site; specify origins/IdP redirects, signed-in proof, sensitive selectors, allowed tools/downloads and timeout. Reuse MB-01 human takeover for sign-in. Adopt source policy of no credential vault in this phase: manifests cannot contain credentials/cookies/tokens/storage state. Keep profile custody private to the authorized conversation, persist only through the approved isolated session owner, enforce at most8h inactivity (or narrower manifest TTL), and destroy/invalidate on expiry, revoked access or explicit logout. Every signed-in interaction receives exact pre-execution approval and durable consumption; it must not be pre-approved merely because the manifest allows the tool. Bound external side effects to named action/target/data, distinguish not-dispatched from unknown, and reconcile exact outcomes before retry.

**Files/owners.** Existing browser/site-workflows/manifest.mjs, session confidentiality/destruction/fence, runtime lifecycle and public browser DTO/approval owner; frontend browser sign-in/permissions/activity; artifact ingest MM-12 for downloads. No replacement password manager, unrestricted OAuth broker or arbitrary remote agent integration.

**Acceptance/tests.** Per-site/IdP native fixtures: sign-in through takeover, MFA/passkey handoff where required, redirects/subresources outside allowlist, confidential field/URL/storage leak canaries, approval reject/expiry/replay/late answer, download exact provenance, browser/host restart, same-conversation continuation versus cross-conversation denial,8h expiry and server-side invalidation including local-storage-token test. Repeat tenant/wall/revocation/cancellation tests on every admitted site. Unknown site version or changed terms/critical flow remains held until requalification.

**Dependencies/unknowns.** MB-01, MT-14, MM-12/13, MR-05/06, MF-18, MX-11/12 and canonical approval owner. Named sites/tasks are a necessary product decision; general completion planning includes this work but cannot invent them or accept terms, sign in or transmit data now.

<a id="mb-03"></a>
#### MB-03 · Required user-device/browser bridge (P2 sequence; contract/authority decision held)

**Requirement/evidence.** P17 M9 L388 explicitly retains the device/browser bridge. Original Greenfield L135 distinguishes cloud browser/files from the user's device; L206 requires a separately estimated scope. The [current browser launch contract](https://github.com/neo-capiro/meri-greenfield/blob/bce707763f2cbabe1a73a99aee7b7ec206fa6996/apps/worker/conversation/browser/README.md) accepts no extension, CDP endpoint or inherited desktop browser state in its ordinary launch. The inspected main apps tree has no demonstrated end-to-end product bridge receiver; this is a missing bounded feature contract, not proof no historical prototype exists.

**Root cause.** A cloud browser cannot supply local device identity, connection consent, local file selection or local authenticated-browser authority. Neither an extension mock nor takeover on a cloud host closes that boundary.

**Bounded implementation.** First freeze supported devices/browsers and the exact initial local read/interaction/file-transfer operations. Select a reviewed adopted transport/extension where suitable rather than rebuilding browser automation. Implement explicit device pairing and revocable identity, authenticated local/cloud session negotiation, lease/expiry, capability intersection and a visible connected/active/paused/disconnected state. User selects local file/destination or exact browser tab; prevent generic filesystem/CDP/credential access. Keep local operation execution on the authorized device boundary, bind every request/receipt to user/tenant/device/session/conversation/run/action/data hash, and enforce current approval before consequential local or remote effects. Require explicit consent for uploads and minimize transmitted data. Preserve audit/retention, cancellation, disconnect and lost-effect reconciliation; reconnect cannot replay a spent request.

**Files/owners.** A bounded bridge adapter under the existing browser runtime boundary plus public device/session/request/receipt DTOs, control authority/currentness and frontend connection/handoff controls. Exact new package/path and any migration IDs are allocated only after the transport design is accepted. Existing MM upload/artifact and MR lifecycle owners consume receipts; no new scheduler, agent loop or blanket connector.

**Acceptance/tests.** Wrong/retired device, stolen/expired pairing reference, user/account switch, second tab/device, device offline mid-read/effect, local permission denied, revoked connection, stale run, duplicate request, unsafe path/symlink, wrong selected file hash, protected authentication material redaction, remote site redirect, cancellation and reconnect with uncertain effect. Test same-tenant different user and cross-tenant denial. Prove a cloud task cannot silently use local files/cookies and a local bridge cannot widen cloud permissions.

**Dependencies/unknowns.** MB-01/02 only when their features are used, MM-12, MR-05–08, MF-18, MX-11/12. Required decisions: platform/transport, minimum local operation scope, managed-device policy, distribution/update/license model, retention and support matrix. This ticket plans the full feature without requesting a device connection or persistent access now.

<a id="mb-04"></a>
#### MB-04 · Complete natural-language revision of a selected immutable artifact version (P1 required v1)

**Requirement/evidence.** P17 M6 L344–352 and original Greenfield L159 require agent-driven revision preserving the original, separate from Office WYSIWYG. Existing [typed revise route and immutable full replacement](https://github.com/neo-capiro/meri-greenfield/blob/bce707763f2cbabe1a73a99aee7b7ec206fa6996/apps/control/document-application/README.md#L16-L32) are real. But M197 [document intent preparer](https://github.com/neo-capiro/meri-greenfield/blob/cf634d4f70376c12ee6dd7b97ec22981dbd4cb2a/apps/control/intents/document-preparer.mjs#L14-L30) fixes baseVersionId:null, and [runtime intent adapter](https://github.com/neo-capiro/meri-greenfield/blob/cf634d4f70376c12ee6dd7b97ec22981dbd4cb2a/apps/runtime/artifacts/intent-preparers.mjs#L71-L88) routes to create. This is a missing conversational revision bridge, not a missing version store.

**Bounded implementation.** Extend existing typed document intent and trusted runtime adapter with an explicit revision subject: selected immutable artifact/version, original source/spec hashes, current permitted reader/writer and exact requested change. Resolve/read original bytes/spec through canonical current-access owners; never let a model/browser supply authority or reinterpret an arbitrary filename as selection. Use the same SDK query to produce the supported full replacement request, then invoke existing /api/documents/revise with exact baseVersionId and stable delivery identity. Preserve original bytes, original/revised source provenance, parent/CAS and current scope. Distinguish human instruction/user-authored content from source-backed compiler assertions. Publish new receipt/version into conversation/history/UI; allow preview/inspect/download/version switch after refresh. On stale head, surface conflict and require explicit new base selection, never silently overwrite/rebase.

**Files/owners.** Existing control/intents/document-preparer and runtime/artifacts/intent-preparers; existing public intent/document contracts and selected-version UI request builder; document-application/revise route and artifact storage remain canonical. Reuse MM-08/11/17/18 and WS-24, not MM-20 editor integration or a new document generator. Core filing remains a separate approved MM-14 action.

**Acceptance/tests.** Select older/newest version, wrong tenant/client/format, suppressed original, concurrent revisions, lost receipt after successful write, retry same delivery, revision cancellation, original bytes unchanged, exact-parent identity, unsupported workbook feature refusal, current-source revocation and all-page/slide/native quality. Reload must show the exact new version once; unknown write must reconcile without regeneration. User can request another change from the resulting version. No original version is destroyed and no revised file is auto-filed/shared.

**Dependencies/unknowns.** MM-08–11/15/17/18; MF-14/16; MT governed intent owner; public schema compatibility. Product decides supported revisions per format without lowering preservation/fidelity. Initial full typed replacement is valid; manual Office editing remains later MM-20.

<a id="mb-05"></a>
#### MB-05 · Complete narrow source-event triggers and truthful routine history (P1 required v1)

**Requirement/evidence.** Original Greenfield L172–173 requires Temporal Schedules AND a narrow endpoint for supported source events, not another scheduler. P17 M5 L334 requires schedule/history/timezone/retry/reauthorization. Current [public routines transport](https://github.com/neo-capiro/meri-greenfield/blob/bce707763f2cbabe1a73a99aee7b7ec206fa6996/apps/control/schedules/application-http.mjs#L7-L12) exposes prepare/approve/list/reconcile, not an admitted event trigger. Existing [Temporal run-history owner](https://github.com/neo-capiro/meri-greenfield/blob/bce707763f2cbabe1a73a99aee7b7ec206fa6996/apps/control/schedules/temporal-run-history.mjs#L18-L33) deliberately reports historyComplete:false/currentRetryVerified:false for bounded original-run references.

**Root cause.** Recurrence support and a partial original-run description do not establish event-trigger authentication/deduplication, complete occurrence/retry history or final destination receipt. Business subscriptions and source-invalidation notifications cannot safely call a generic model-run endpoint.

**Bounded implementation.** Define a finite producer/event-type/version allowlist with authenticated producer identity, canonical source/client/resource revision, event identity/hash/time and approved subscription/routine reference. Server derives initiating owner/current scope/destination/budget/retry policy from the durable approved routine; untrusted event payload carries no grant or free prompt. Persist event acceptance/deduplication and causal link before invoking the existing occurrence/Temporal owner. Reauthorize membership, source consent and action grants at every execution; suppress revoked/disabled/superseded events. Specify ordering, coalescing, replay window, burst backpressure, retry/poison events and exact unknown-ACK recovery. Use SQS/EventBridge only for a specific accepted source transport, never as a second run scheduler.

Extend existing routine history projection/public DTO and MF consumer to explain event→accepted/skipped/deduped/queued→occurrence/attempt→approval→terminal result/artifact/destination, with retry/continued-run links, cursors and explicit partial coverage. A notification delivered or event accepted is not a successful job. Bound retention of event payloads and receipts under MR-05; keep hashes/content-free lineage where content is suppressed.

**Files/owners.** Existing apps/control/schedules/{application,scheduled-occurrence,scheduled-dispatch,scheduled-job-lineage,temporal-run-history,postgres-schedule-owner}; one source-trigger receiver and contract under the same owner; centrally allocated additive durable schema; existing runtime schedule product port and frontend routine history. MR-03/04/10 remain canonical management/consent/firing/history owners. MO-06 business subscriptions and MO-14 source-revision invalidation consume this contract; they do not implement their own scheduler.

**Acceptance/tests.** Duplicate/redelivered/out-of-order event, wrong producer/tenant/client/source version, expired subscription/current grant, disabled schedule, burst/overlap, source deletion during queue, UTC/DST where scheduled windows apply, worker death, lost event-accept/dispatch ACK, held approval, unknown external effect and poisoned event recovery. Exactly one admitted occurrence for the deduplication key; no second effect from replay. History covers original and current retry/continuation truthfully or returns explicit partial/unknown, survives originating chat closure/reload and hides revoked source content.

**Dependencies/unknowns.** MR-03–05/09/10, MT-12, MF-18, MO-06/14, MX-04/11/12. First producer/event types, acceptable lag/burst/replay policy and destination types require a bounded contract decision. No live webhook registration, source subscription or recurring automation is created by this plan.

### 5. Amend existing owners without duplicating projects

- **MT-13:** all14 named skill workflows mandatory. A bounded early cohort is only an interim milestone; exact version/evidence/completion/approval/recovery acceptance remains for every required ID.
- **MM-12:** include adopted pypdf/native Office reads and Tesseract scanned-image OCR where required, with file/page/time limits, provenance, OCR uncertainty and no silent empty-text success. Preserve originals and current source scope. No new extraction engine.
- **MM-20:** required full-program Office editor remains on the ledger after first live/desktop-v1 sequencing. It is distinct from MB-04 natural-language revision and remains blocked until its existing adoption/license/native/session/retention gates pass.
- **MF-20/21:** explicitly retain responsive tablet/phone and light/dark frames plus keyboard/accessibility as a later required completion cohort. Desktop-only acceptance does not close full responsive/mobile scope. Exact device/viewports and deferred date are decisions; “deferred/decision-held” is not “done.”
- **PF-24/WS-22 plus current Objective owner:** preserve P17 C2/M7 issuer, protected-facts custody/replay/readback, correct crypto-purpose boundaries, native DB privileges/trigger/RLS, exact consumer source versions and server-owned completion after browser close. Do not revive superseded Meri Planner or duplicate Core workflow ownership.
- **MX-09–14:** retain every original quantitative, security, recovery, adoption and operator gate above. In particular mandatory real200-case reliability work and 60-task success/correctness cohorts are not replaced by current scripted60 slots; zero unqualified native/runtime receipts are promoted.

### 6. Closure and handoff

The historical crosswalk has53 requirements,139 native rows and14 skills; all53 requirements and all18 release criteria are mapped here. All81 Oct17 paired-C2/M1–M9/Q checkbox items have an owner. The existing tools per-operation/exclusion tables remain canonical for96 selected/43 excluded. Five explicit missing-feature tickets close the newly identified under-bounded surfaces; other scope remains with the parent original-plan, Oct20 MO, and existing MF/MR/MT/MM/MX/PF/WS/ST/ML owners.

This report is not a completion claim. No requirement is closed merely because it has a ticket. For each original mandatory/accepted feature, full completion requires current source and public contract, authorized durable behavior, actual UI/receipt/history and later access, retention/recovery, native qualification and the applicable frozen live acceptance. Required later milestones remain open until their own checks pass or the user explicitly changes their scope.

## October20 remaining requirements and full-product closure tickets

### Scope and evidence rule

The user's later clarification is “will this plan incldue the original plan? essentially everything has to be done to fully complete meri?” and “every featurte?”. Consequently initial-release exclusions and held capability flags are not scope deletion. Every original product behavior below remains tracked to a canonical completion owner; implementation may be staged and activation gated, but a smaller launch cannot be called full completion. Historical aliases may consolidate only when their user-visible behavior, authority and result semantics survive. Superseded architecture means replace obsolete mechanics, not discard the feature.

This is a read-only reconciliation, not implementation, deployment authorization or a live-completion receipt. The Remaining Plan's assumption that the Adjustment Plan was completed was a premise of that dated analysis. It does not prove today's code or deployment passes the same obligations. Current MF/MR/MT/MM/MX tickets remain canonical; MO tickets below add uncovered original requirements, not duplicate provider/runtime projects. P1 deadline and P2 authority-proof owners remain separate and undisturbed.

Status legend: **EXISTING** = current canonical ticket already owns closure, not a claim it passed; **EXPAND** = new MO requirement or added acceptance to an existing ticket; **CONDITIONAL** = retained feature/technical option with original trigger and named owner, not removed; **SUPERSEDED-MECHANICS** = obsolete architecture replaced while behavior remains; **NOT-VERIFIED** = current source/live evidence cannot close requirement. No original row is silently marked complete.
